@@1105659 <h> Penny wise and pound-foolish : reducing ozone science when we need it most <h> On the 25th anniversary of the Montreal Protocol , which banned ozone-destroying refrigerants , top ozone scientists from around the world are gathering in Toronto . They will put heat on Canada 's Environment Minister , Peter Kent to stop un-funding ozone research in the north . <p> Ozone hole that opened over the Antarctic in 2000 was largest to that date . In 2011 a hole opened over the arctic for the first time . <p> By:Sultan Latif and Janos Mate Published on Tue Aug 28 2012 <p> Would it make sense to ask a doctor to examine a patient , but take away their stethoscope ? That is what the Canadian government is asking scientists to do when it comes to ' examining ' the ozone layer . <p> Many assume that the ozone problem was fixed in the 1980s . In reality , the annual Antarctic ozone hole keeps reappearing at near record sizes and last year , for the first time , scientists reported an ozone hole over the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the northern hemisphere last year , Environment Canada decided to drastically reduce its ozone science and monitoring program . It closed several of Canada 's 17 ozone monitoring stations , stations which have provided the longest-running record of ozone levels in the world . They are about one-third of the Arctic ozone measurements . <p> In 1985 , the British Antarctic Survey discovered the Antarctic ozone hole through routine ground level ozone monitoring , conducted to measure the density of the ozone layer . NASA 's atmospheric scientists say satellite imagery had missed the near total depletion of the ozone layer over Antarctica . <p> Satellite imagery and data collection did not -- and can not -- give a complete picture of the stratospheric ozone layer because of the angle of the sun 's light over the Arctic and Antarctic . Ground level ozone monitoring is essential to cross-reference the satellite data and provide a full understanding of it . In-situ measurements are essential for validating satellite measurements , asserts Johannes Staehelin , chair of the World Meteorological Organization 's ozone science advisory group . <p> Following the alarming discovery of @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Protocol . It implemented a gradual phase-out regime of major ozone depleting chemicals used in aerosols , refrigerants , insulation foam propellants , fire retardants and agricultural fumigants . <p> With every country in the world signed on , the 1987 Montreal Protocol is still recognized as the most successful international environmental treaty to date . It was successful because it was based in the most accurate and complete scientific data available , and has put the ozone layer on the track to potential recovery . But to ensure that and remain vigilant , we need exact ozone science . <p> Closures in the ozone monitoring program may " lead to an erosion of the Montreal Protocol , which obliges Arctic countries to monitor the ozone layer and maintain scientific ozone research , " says Markus Rex , a former atmospheric scientist and now with the Alfred Wegener Institute for Polar and Marine Research . <p> Last month , Harvard University scientists confirmed a further link between climate change and ozone layer depletion . Their study states that more intense storms , caused by global warming , via convection , thrust @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ possible . This creates atmospheric conditions which could lead to further , substantial ozone loss , making the connection between global warming and further ozone layer depletion more apparent ( if not yet fully understood ) . <p> With the biggest Arctic footprint in the world , Canada is asserting its sovereignty over it , while significantly reducing science 's stethoscope to monitor the state of the ozone layer above it . <p> Canada 's ozone-monitoring network has collected key in situ data used all over the world showing the unprecedented depletion of stratospheric ozone over the Arctic in 2011 . Closures within the Canadian ozone-monitoring network are " devastating for the whole field " says Tom Duck , an atmospheric researcher at Dalhousie University . <p> On the 25th anniversary of the Montreal Protocol , top ozone scientists from around the world are gathering in Toronto this week for the Quadrennial Ozone Symposium in Toronto . My forthcoming book also discusses the Conservatives ' bombing of Libya , ongoing war in Afghanistan and support for aggression in Iran , Lebanon and Somalia . ( This is an entirely doable project . @@1109978 <h> Eighteen UBC students win prestigious Vanier Canada Scholarship <p> August 30 , 2012 <p> Eighteen doctoral students from the University of British Columbia have been awarded the 2012 Vanier Canada Scholarships . Vanier scholars receive $50,000 annually for up to three years to support their graduate studies in the social sciences , humanities , natural sciences , engineering or health sciences . <p> Joanna Bernhardt , a researcher at UBC and now a Vanier scholar , is exploring coastal ecosystems in the face of climate change . Coastal ecosystems provide recreational opportunities , protection from storms and healthy seafood . <p> Bernhardt 's research will involve experiments in naturally occurring ocean " cold spots " to gather information on resilience to climate warming . This study will advance the science needed to support the marine policies that will help sustain the coastal ecosystems over the next century and beyond . <p> " I 'm honoured to be included among the highly talented and engaged group of Vanier scholars , " said Bernhardt . " As a marine ecologist , I believe strongly in doing use-inspired research in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ will give me both the financial freedom to tackle questions about how to best manage our valuable oceans in the face of climate change and the time to apply this science and learn from the communities in which I work . " <p> UBC has the second highest number of scholars , after the University of Toronto . In today 's federal government announcement , 156 Vanier scholars from 29 universities were named , including graduate students from the United States , Europe , Asia and Africa who are studying in Canada . <p> The 2012 scholarships are valued at $23.4 million . Since the program 's inception in 2008 , 660 Vanier scholarships have been awarded -- a federal investment of $98.4 million . <p> Vanier scholars are selected through a competitive process , administered by Canada 's three research granting councils : the Social Sciences and Humanities Research Council , the Natural Sciences and Engineering Research Council , and the Canadian Institutes of Health Research .

@@1112065 <p> " It expands the walls of the classroom , it goes beyond the blackboard and gets ( students ) out there using their senses as they learn , " says Jenny Guibert , one of two project managers for the guide and an education instructor at Nipissing 's Brantford campus . <p> " It 's how kids get that instant connection with nature . " <p> In an era when " nature deficit disorder " has become a buzz phrase and children spend more time in the virtual world that the real one , getting outdoors each day is more important than ever . <p> That 's why the 16 lesson plans -- aligned with the Ontario curriculum -- include activities like a walkabout in the schoolyard to identify nature , and treks through the neighbourhood to find sources of water , pollinators and to track how many people are walking , cycling , driving or waiting at bus stops . <p> These then become springboards for discussing topics like biodiversity , water conservation and green transportation . <p> The approach reflects a shift in thinking among @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , who co-managed the project with Guibert . <p> Until recently , the focus was on teaching kids about issues like recycling and climate change . Now , it 's on cultivating a love of nature first , and helping children recognize and appreciate it exists . <p> " That has to be the starting point , especially with that age group , " says Clare . " You want to build on that sense of wonder . " <p> Once kids embrace nature and understand how everything is interconnected , they 're much more likely to be passionate about protecting it . <p> The Connecting with Nature project was launched in 2010 when the Suzuki Foundation decided to update its 2006 teaching guide . <p> The first guide alone was born out of more than 2,000 volunteer hours and had input from 600 student teachers . <p> Similar guides for junior kindergarten to Grade 3 , and Grades 7 and 8 should be ready for next year . <p> The Toronto District School Board is enthusiastic because it fits with their initiatives to promote " eco-schools " and recent plans @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , " says board superintendent Jeff Hainbuch . <p> He calls it " a win-win " that combines the expertise of teachers and the credibility of Suzuki to get kids excited about the outdoor environment . <p> " You ca n't learn about that connectedness just from a textbook , " he says . " But you do n't have to go to an outdoor ed centre to do that either . " <p> Jordan Tamblyn remembers what it 's like to be a kid catching frogs , picking flowers and exploring the woods near her cottage . " This lets them . " <p> A year ago , she witnessed how quickly kids engage while piloting one of the lessons she created at a camp . In this file photo : The Stop UBC Animal Research group teamed up with animal rights advocates PETA , staging a mock research laboratory in Vancouver , B.C. , in front of Vancouver Art Gallery on Oct. 24 , 2010 . <h> Photograph by : Ian Smith , PNG <p> The University of B.C. is improving oversight of its animal research and has upgraded various facilities , the institution said as @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ meet voluntary national standards in these areas . <p> The Canadian Council on Animal Care found " deficiencies " at UBC , including a need for more oversight of studies by the university 's Animal Care Committee , which includes university scientists and community members . <p> The university said the report was being released today to be more transparent about its treatment of research animals . In 2010 , 60 animal advocacy groups across Canada , the U.S. and Europe sent a letter to UBC demanding " its veil of secrecy surrounding animal research " be lifted . <p> The 21-month-old report called on the university to create a better system of tracking animals , including the numbers of animals used and whether they were bought , caught in the wild or bred . The council said the records should be consistently checked against the numbers of animals in approved studies . <p> The report also recommended the university upgrade buildings where animals are housed and experiments conducted ; ensure animal users , including students , are adequately trained ; and increase veterinary services . <p> For example , during @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ found housing for rabbits , song sparrows , pigs , monkeys , sheep and a llama was inadequate . <p> The report noted the wild-caught sparrows in particular seemed stressed by their captive conditions . That building - the Animal Care Centre Annex - was replaced in 2011 . <p> In another example , the council found a research facility housing about 1,100 cages of mice , rats , guinea pigs , and " non-human primates " was old and poorly designed and did n't meet guidelines . UBC said this facility is scheduled for renovation this year . The building 's name was blacked out in the report due to safety concerns , the university said . <p> The council on animal care is a peer-review body whose recommendations on the ethical use of animals are n't binding . However , the council 's approval is important in securing research funding . <p> Helen Burt , UBC 's associate vice-president of research , said centralizing animal research in three new , large facilities , is a major step to addressing the problem of oversight and tracking . <p> The university @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ for which it received a commendation from the council . Since 2004 , the number of facilities has dropped to 22 from 34 . <p> It was much harder to have oversight of " lost , satellite " operations , noted Burt . <p> UBC is also planning to buy a computer database system to better track animals . <p> The university has increased the number of veterinarians on staff to five , from one in 2005 , and may add a sixth . <p> " There clearly is a lot of work to do , but we are moving absolutely in the right direction , " said Burt . " ( Releasing the report ) will hopefully demonstrate to the public the level of commitment both on the national level and institutional level to doing things right - to humane care , to responsible use of animals , and levels of compliance with standards . " <p> The 21-month-old report was being released now because UBC only recently decided to be more transparent , said Burt . <p> The report from an expected council assessment in 2013 will likely be @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , UBC revealed that more than 200,000 animals were used for research purposes in 2010 , 98 per cent of which were rodents . Free Prior and Informed Consent is written into today 's United Nations Declaration of the Rights of Indigenous Peoples . This would increase tanker traffic @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ weather conditions , and many small islands which leave little room for large tankers to maneuver ) Douglas Channel , as well as expand B.C.
